Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says.     "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I came in because I just... I feel like my brain keeps stalling out. That’s the best way I can describe it. Like at work, I’ll have a test report open, or even something simple like a CAD note that should just be a quick sentence or checking a box, and I’ll sit there staring at it for, I don’t know, an hour sometimes. And it’s not that I don’t understand what it’s asking, exactly. It’s more like I can’t make my mind move to the next step.

I keep thinking, okay, just write the next sentence, just decide which checkbox fits, just finish this one tiny part, and then nothing happens. I get stuck on something that used to be automatic. It feels embarrassing because it’s not complicated engineering work at that point—it’s basic documentation—and I still can’t seem to decide what to put down. So… yeah, that’s why I’m here. Something feels really wrong with how slow and stuck my thinking has gotten.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… definitely. That’s actually one of the things that feels kind of sad, because it’s not like I don’t have things I used to like. I do. I have a resin printer for miniatures, and I used to get really into it—setting up the model, cleaning it up, painting little details, all of that. But there’s been the same half-finished figure just sitting there for months. I see it and think, “I should finish that,” but it doesn’t feel exciting. It just feels like another unfinished obligation sitting in the room.

And it’s not only that. I used to do yoga or go walk in the woods when I needed to clear my head, and those were things I actually wanted to do before. Now even that feels like… effort. Like another task on a checklist. I’ll think about going for a walk and instead of feeling any relief or interest, it feels like I’m being assigned something. So, yes, I’ve noticed that. A lot of things that used to feel relaxing or enjoyable just don’t really feel that way right now.

manifestation: I keep replaying tiny mistakes from the lab and telling myself I'm not just bad at this job but bad as a person, like I deserve whatever consequences come from it.\n- key: S032\n  name: didn't arrive anywhere, and sometimes the idea of steering off the road scares me enough that I grip the wheel and pull over.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur exclusively during the major depressive episode\n  manifestation: I never had experiences like hearing voices in the lab or feeling certain people were building a case against me before this depression started after the role change. They only show up when I am in this low, guilty state.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  manifestation: I missed a key project deadline and now need coworkers to double-check or take over parts of reports I used to finish on my own. Outside work, I have mostly stopped seeing people or doing the routines that kept me steady.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 1 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on since I was moved into the prototype-testing role about a year and a month ago.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 25 / engineering_technologist_or_technician / bachelors\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S031\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I just... I feel like my brain keeps stalling out. That’s the best way I can describe it. Like at work, I’ll have a test report open, or even something simple like a CAD note that should just be a quick sentence or checking a box, and I’ll sit there staring at it for, I don’t know, an hour sometimes. I don’t really know. I mean, I feel tired, but I’m not sure if it’s the kind of tired you mean. It’s hard to separate actual physical tiredness from just… everything feeling like it takes more effort than it should.

Like, I wouldn’t say I’m collapsing or unable to get up, exactly. I still do things. But there’s this sense that I’m dragging myself through them, and even small things feel like they require a lot of gearing up. Sometimes by the time I’ve gotten through the basic workday or even just tried to focus on something for a while, I feel kind of used up.

But I don’t know if I’ve noticed a clear “lack of energy most days” in a way I can confidently say yes to. It’s more like I’m slower, or everything has more friction than it used to. So… maybe? But I’m not completely sure.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… yes, I think so. More than usual, definitely.

It’s mostly around work, especially things from the lab. I’ll make some tiny mistake, or even just think maybe I handled something awkwardly or didn’t notice something fast enough, and then I’ll replay it over and over after I leave. Not just in a normal “I should do better next time” way. It turns into this whole thing where I’m telling myself I’m bad at the job, and then somehow it becomes bigger than that, like I’m not just bad at the job, I’m bad as a person.

And I know that probably sounds dramatic, because some of the things are really small. Like things other people might correct and move on from. But I can’t seem to move on from them. I keep thinking about what I should’ve done differently, what someone must think of me, what consequences I deserve for messing up. It’s like I start feeling like if something bad happens because of it, then that’s just what I get.

So… yes. I’ve been blaming myself a lot, and it feels heavier than just being self-critical.

I’m not really sure. I haven’t been tracking my weight or anything, and I don’t think I’ve noticed some obvious change like my clothes fitting completely differently.

It’s been… longer than just a few weeks. I think the clearest marker for me is when I got moved into the prototype-testing role at work. That was about a year and a month ago now.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I think so. I didn’t really notice it at first as “slowed down,” exactly, but people at work have started doing this thing where they finish my sentences in meetings. Like I’ll know what I’m trying to say, or I’ll be trying to answer a question about a test run, and there’s this delay where the words just don’t come out fast enough. And then someone jumps in and says what they think I mean, and sometimes they’re right, but it makes me feel… I don’t know, embarrassed, like I’m taking up too much time just trying to talk.

And moving around feels slower too. Even walking from my desk to the test chamber feels like I’m moving through thick syrup. That sounds weird, but it’s the closest way I can describe it. It’s not that I physically can’t walk, it’s more like every step takes more effort than it should, and I’m aware of how slow I am. Like everything that should be automatic has this drag on it now.

So yes, I think other people are noticing the speech part, at least. And I’m definitely noticing the movement part.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I think that’s been happening. I feel like I already kind of said it, but it’s one of the more obvious things because other people seem to notice it before I do.

In meetings, especially, I’ll be trying to answer something and it’s like there’s this pause between knowing what I mean and actually getting the words out. People have started finishing my sentences for me, which is… really uncomfortable. They don’t even always do it in a rude way, I think they’re just trying to keep things moving, but it makes me feel like I’m taking too long to function normally.

And physically, yes, I feel slower too. Even walking from my desk to the test chamber feels like I’m pushing through something thick, like syrup or mud. It’s not that I can’t move, exactly. It’s more like every movement takes this extra effort and I’m aware of how sluggish I am. So yes, I’d say my speech and movement both feel noticeably slower than usual.

I used to do yoga or go walk in the woods when I needed to clear my head, and those were things I actually wanted to do before. Now even that feels like… effort. Like another task on a checklist. A lot of things that used to feel relaxing or enjoyable just don’t really feel that way right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, depressed, or hopeless most days lately?\n\nPatient: I’m… not totally sure how to answer that cleanly. I don’t know if I’d use the word “hopeless,” exactly, or at least I don’t know if that’s the word I’d reach for first. “Down” maybe, but even then it’s hard for me to tell whether I’m actually sad most of the time or whether I’m just kind of worn out and frustrated by how everything feels.\n\nIt’s not like I wake up every morning thinking one clear thought like, “I’m depressed.” It’s more like there’s this heaviness around things, and I notice I’m not really looking forward to much. But I don’t know if that counts as feeling down most days, or if it’s just me being overwhelmed. I guess I’d say I haven’t been feeling like myself, but I’m not sure I can label it better than that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling tired or noticed that you have a lack of energy most days lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, I feel tired, but I’m not sure if it’s the kind of tired you mean. It’s hard to separate actual physical tiredness from just… everything feeling like it takes more effort than it should.\n\nLike, I wouldn’t say I’m collapsing or unable to get up, exactly. I still do things. But there’s this sense that I’m dragging myself through them, and even small things feel like they require a lot of gearing up. Sometimes by the time I’ve gotten through the basic workday or even just tried to focus on something for a while, I feel kind of used up.\n\nBut I don’t know if I’ve noticed a clear “lack of energy most days” in a way I can confidently say yes to. It’s more like I’m slower, or everything has more friction than it used to. So… maybe? I haven’t been tracking my weight or anything, and I don’t think I’ve noticed some obvious change like my clothes fitting completely differently.\n\nWith appetite… it’s a little hard to say because my eating is kind of inconsistent anyway depending on the day and how busy I am. I wouldn’t say there’s been a clear pattern of eating a lot more or a lot less that I can point to.\n\nSo… I guess no major change that I’ve noticed, or at least nothing I feel confident saying is a real change.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way? Yeah… very much. I feel like I spend a lot of time replaying these tiny mistakes from the lab, things that probably other people would just correct and move on from, but I can’t seem to let them go.

Like if I entered something wrong, or missed a small detail during a test setup, or even just answered a question awkwardly, I’ll go over it again and again afterward. And it doesn’t stay as, “I made a mistake.” It turns into, “I’m bad at this job,” and then pretty quickly into, “I’m just bad, as a person.” Like it says something rotten about me, not just about my work.

Like I deserve whatever consequences come from it, even if the consequence is people being angry with me or deciding I shouldn’t be there anymore. I keep thinking, “Well, that would be fair.” It’s hard to explain, but yes, I’ve been blaming myself a lot more than I used to.

But I don’t know if that counts as feeling down most days, or if it’s just me being overwhelmed. I guess I’d say I haven’t been feeling like myself, but I’m not sure I can label it better than that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling tired or noticed that you have a lack of energy most days lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, I feel tired, but I’m not sure if it’s the kind of tired you mean. It’s hard to separate actual physical tiredness from just… everything feeling like it takes more effort than it should.\n\nLike, I wouldn’t say I’m collapsing or unable to get up, exactly. I still do things. But there’s this sense that I’m dragging myself through them, and even small things feel like they require a lot of gearing up. Sometimes by the time I’ve gotten through the basic workday or even just tried to focus on something for a while, I feel kind of used up.\n\nBut I don’t know if I’ve noticed a clear “lack of energy most days” in a way I can confidently say yes to. It’s more like I’m slower, or everything has more friction than it used to. So… maybe? But I’m not completely sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you’re not good enough or blamed yourself for things more than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I think so. More than usual, definitely.\n\nIt’s mostly around work, especially things from the lab. I’ll make some tiny mistake, or even just think maybe I handled something awkwardly or didn’t notice something fast enough, and then I’ll replay it over and over after I leave. Not just in a normal “I should do better next time” way. It turns into this whole thing where I’m telling myself I’m bad at the job, and then somehow it becomes bigger than that, like I’m not just bad at the job, I’m bad as a person.\n\nAnd I know that probably sounds dramatic, because some of the things are really small. Like things other people might correct and move on from. But I can’t seem to move on from them. I keep thinking about what I should’ve done differently, what someone must think of me, what consequences I deserve for messing up. It’s like I start feeling like if something bad happens because of it, then that’s just what I get.\n\nSo… yes. I’ve been blaming myself a lot, and it feels heavier than just being self-critical.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight lately—like eating more or less than usual or losing or gaining weight without trying?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t been tracking my weight or anything, and I don’t think I’ve noticed some obvious change like my clothes fitting completely differently.\n\nWith appetite… it’s a little hard to say because my eating is kind of inconsistent anyway depending on the day and how busy I am. Sometimes I’ll realize I didn’t eat much earlier, but I don’t know if that’s new or if I’m just paying more attention because you’re asking. Other times I eat normally enough. I wouldn’t say there’s been a clear pattern of eating a lot more or a lot less that I can point to.\n\nSo… I guess no major change that I’ve noticed, or at least nothing I feel confident saying is a real change.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way? Has it been going on for weeks, months, or longer?\n\nPatient: It’s been… longer than just a few weeks. I think the clearest marker for me is when I got moved into the prototype-testing role at work. That was about a year and a month ago now.\n\nI don’t know if everything changed overnight exactly, but that’s when I remember this all really starting to feel like it settled in and didn’t lift. Before that, I had bad days, obviously, but after that move it started feeling more constant, like this became the background of my life instead of just a rough patch.\n\nSo, yeah… I’d say it’s been going on for about thirteen months.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your movements or speech have slowed down lately, like other people might comment that you're moving or talking more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I didn’t really notice it at first as “slowed down,” exactly, but people at work have started doing this thing where they finish my sentences in meetings. Like I’ll know what I’m trying to say, or I’ll be trying to answer a question about a test run, and there’s this delay where the words just don’t come out fast enough. And then someone jumps in and says what they think I mean, and sometimes they’re right, but it makes me feel… I don’t know, embarrassed, like I’m taking up too much time just trying to talk.\n\nAnd moving around feels slower too. Even walking from my desk to the test chamber feels like I’m moving through thick syrup. That sounds weird, but it’s the closest way I can describe it. It’s not that I physically can’t walk, it’s more like every step takes more effort than it should, and I’m aware of how slow I am. Like everything that should be automatic has this drag on it now.\n\nSo yes, I think other people are noticing the speech part, at least. And I’m definitely noticing the movement part.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your movements or speech have become noticeably slower or more sluggish than usual, to the point where others might comment on it?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s been happening. I feel like I already kind of said it, but it’s one of the more obvious things because other people seem to notice it before I do.\n\nIn meetings, especially, I’ll be trying to answer something and it’s like there’s this pause between knowing what I mean and actually getting the words out. People have started finishing my sentences for me, which is… really uncomfortable. They don’t even always do it in a rude way, I think they’re just trying to keep things moving, but it makes me feel like I’m taking too long to function normally.\n\nAnd physically, yes, I feel slower too. Even walking from my desk to the test chamber feels like I’m pushing through something thick, like syrup or mud. It’s not that I can’t move, exactly. It’s more like every movement takes this extra effort and I’m aware of how sluggish I am. So yes, I’d say my speech and movement both feel noticeably slower than usual.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you’re not good enough, or blamed yourself for things more than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… very much. I feel like I spend a lot of time replaying these tiny mistakes from the lab, things that probably other people would just correct and move on from, but I can’t seem to let them go.\n\nLike if I entered something wrong, or missed a small detail during a test setup, or even just answered a question awkwardly, I’ll go over it again and again afterward. And it doesn’t stay as, “I made a mistake.” It turns into, “I’m bad at this job,” and then pretty quickly into, “I’m just bad, as a person.” Like it says something rotten about me, not just about my work.\n\nAnd I know that probably sounds extreme when I say it out loud, because some of the things are really small. But in my head they don’t feel small. They feel like proof. Like I deserve whatever consequences come from it, even if the consequence is people being angry with me or deciding I shouldn’t be there anymore. I keep thinking, “Well, that would be fair.” It’s hard to explain, but yes, I’ve been blaming myself a lot more than I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately, like trouble falling asleep, waking up frequently during the night, or waking up earlier than usual and not being able to go back to sleep?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure how to answer that, honestly. I mean… I sleep, but I haven’t been tracking it or anything, so it’s hard for me to say if it’s changed in some clear pattern.\n\nSome nights feel worse than others, but I don’t know if that’s because I’m actually waking up more or because I’m just more aware of the time passing. I’ll look at the clock and feel like I’ve been lying there forever, but then I can’t always tell if I was awake the whole time or sort of half-asleep. It’s kind of blurry.\n\nI don’t think I’ve noticed one specific thing like always waking up at the same early hour or anything like that. And I’m not sure I’d say I consistently can’t fall asleep. It’s more that my sleep doesn’t feel very dependable, if that makes sense. But I don’t have a clean answer like, “yes, I wake up three times every night.” I just… haven’t paid close enough attention to say.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. And it doesn’t stay as, “I made a mistake.” It turns into, “I’m bad at this job,” and then pretty quickly into, “I’m just bad, as a person.” Like it says something rotten about me, not just about my work.\n\nAnd I know that probably sounds extreme when I say it out loud, because some of the things are really small. I don’t think so… not in a way I can clearly point to. I mean, I wouldn’t say food has been especially appealing or anything, but I also haven’t noticed myself just not eating or dropping meals on purpose.

But I don’t know if that counts as feeling down most days, or if it’s just me being overwhelmed. I mean, I feel tired, but I’m not sure if it’s the kind of tired you mean. I still do things. But there’s this sense that I’m dragging myself through them, and even small things feel like they require a lot of gearing up. Sometimes by the time I’ve gotten through the basic workday or even just tried to focus on something for a while, I feel kind of used up.\n\nBut I don’t know if I’ve noticed a clear “lack of energy most days” in a way I can confidently say yes to. It’s more like I’m slower, or everything has more friction than it used to. So… maybe? I’ve been blaming myself a lot, and it feels heavier than just being self-critical.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight lately—like eating more or less than usual or losing or gaining weight without trying?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t been tracking my weight or anything, and I don’t think I’ve noticed some obvious change like my clothes fitting completely differently.\n\nWith appetite… it’s a little hard to say because my eating is kind of inconsistent anyway depending on the day and how busy I am. Sometimes I’ll realize I didn’t eat much earlier, but I don’t know if that’s new or if I’m just paying more attention because you’re asking. Other times I eat normally enough. I wouldn’t say there’s been a clear pattern of eating a lot more or a lot less that I can point to.\n\nSo… I guess no major change that I’ve noticed, or at least nothing I feel confident saying is a real change.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way? Before that, I had bad days, obviously, but after that move it started feeling more constant, like this became the background of my life instead of just a rough patch.\n\nSo, yeah… I’d say it’s been going on for about thirteen months.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your movements or speech have slowed down lately, like other people might comment that you're moving or talking more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I didn’t really notice it at first as “slowed down,” exactly, but people at work have started doing this thing where they finish my sentences in meetings. And then someone jumps in and says what they think I mean, and sometimes they’re right, but it makes me feel… I don’t know, embarrassed, like I’m taking up too much time just trying to talk.\n\nAnd moving around feels slower too. They don’t even always do it in a rude way, I think they’re just trying to keep things moving, but it makes me feel like I’m taking too long to function normally.\n\nAnd physically, yes, I feel slower too. Even walking from my desk to the test chamber feels like I’m pushing through something thick, like syrup or mud. It’s not that I can’t move, exactly. It’s more like every movement takes this extra effort and I’m aware of how sluggish I am. So yes, I’d say my speech and movement both feel noticeably slower than usual.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you’re not good enough, or blamed yourself for things more than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… very much. It’s actually been more the opposite, I think. I don’t feel jittery exactly, or like I need to pace. It’s more like everything in my body has gotten… slowed down.

People have started finishing my sentences for me, or jumping in because there’s this awkward pause where I’m just sitting there trying to make my mouth catch up. If anything, I feel physically slowed down, and I think other people can see it.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you been feeling like you’re worthless or felt guilty about things more than usual lately?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 25 / engineering_technologist_or_technician / bachelors\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Worthlessness_Guilt\nmanifestation: \"I keep replaying tiny mistakes from the lab and telling myself I'm not just bad at this job but bad as a person, like I deserve whatever consequences come from it.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… yes. That’s probably one of the things I notice the most, actually.

It’s not just like, “Oh, I made a mistake, that was embarrassing.” It turns into this whole thing in my head where I keep replaying really small stuff from the lab over and over. Like if I labeled something awkwardly, or missed a step and had to correct it, or asked a question I feel like I should’ve already known the answer to… I’ll think about it later and it feels huge. Way bigger than it probably looked to anyone else.

And then I don’t just think, “I’m having a hard time,” or “I need to improve.” I start telling myself I’m bad at the job, and then it kind of keeps going until it’s like… I’m not just bad at the job, I’m bad as a person. Like there’s something fundamentally wrong with me. And if there are consequences, or if people get frustrated with me, part of me feels like I deserve it. Like whatever happens because of it is just what I should expect.

I know that sounds extreme when I say it out loud, especially because some of the mistakes are tiny. But in the moment, it doesn’t feel tiny. It feels like proof.

So yes, I’d say my speech and movement both feel noticeably slower than usual.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you’re not good enough, or blamed yourself for things more than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… very much. I feel like I spend a lot of time replaying these tiny mistakes from the lab, things that probably other people would just correct and move on from, but I can’t seem to let them go.\n\nLike if I entered something wrong, or missed a small detail during a test setup, or even just answered a question awkwardly, I’ll go over it again and again afterward. And it doesn’t stay as, “I made a mistake.” It turns into, “I’m bad at this job,” and then pretty quickly into, “I’m just bad, as a person.” Like it says something rotten about me, not just about my work.\n\nAnd I know that probably sounds extreme when I say it out loud, because some of the things are really small. But in my head they don’t feel small. They feel like proof. Like I deserve whatever consequences come from it, even if the consequence is people being angry with me or deciding I shouldn’t be there anymore. I keep thinking, “Well, that would be fair.” It’s hard to explain, but yes, I’ve been blaming myself a lot more than I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately, like trouble falling asleep, waking up frequently during the night, or waking up earlier than usual and not being able to go back to sleep?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure how to answer that, honestly. I mean… I sleep, but I haven’t been tracking it or anything, so it’s hard for me to say if it’s changed in some clear pattern.\n\nSome nights feel worse than others, but I don’t know if that’s because I’m actually waking up more or because I’m just more aware of the time passing. I’ll look at the clock and feel like I’ve been lying there forever, but then I can’t always tell if I was awake the whole time or sort of half-asleep. It’s kind of blurry.\n\nI don’t think I’ve noticed one specific thing like always waking up at the same early hour or anything like that. And I’m not sure I’d say I consistently can’t fall asleep. It’s more that my sleep doesn’t feel very dependable, if that makes sense. But I don’t have a clean answer like, “yes, I wake up three times every night.” I just… haven’t paid close enough attention to say.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself losing interest in food or experiencing a decrease in appetite to the point where you’re eating significantly less than usual, even if it’s not intentional?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a way I can clearly point to. I mean, I wouldn’t say food has been especially appealing or anything, but I also haven’t noticed myself just not eating or dropping meals on purpose.\n\nMy eating is a little hard to judge because my schedule can be uneven, and sometimes I’ll realize I waited too long between meals, but that’s not really new enough or dramatic enough for me to say, “yes, my appetite has decreased.” I don’t think I’ve been eating significantly less than usual. At least, not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself feeling restless, jittery, or unable to sit still lately, to the point where you move around more than usual or feel physically uncomfortable staying in one place?\n\nPatient: It’s actually been more the opposite, I think. I don’t feel jittery exactly, or like I need to pace. It’s more like everything in my body has gotten… slowed down.\n\nAt work, it’s started to be noticeable, which is embarrassing. In meetings, when someone asks me something, I know I should be able to answer, and sometimes I even have the answer in my head, but getting the words out takes so long. People have started finishing my sentences for me, or jumping in because there’s this awkward pause where I’m just sitting there trying to make my mouth catch up. And then I feel even worse because it looks like I don’t know what I’m doing.\n\nEven walking around feels strange. Like going from my desk to the test chamber isn’t far, but lately it feels like I’m moving through thick syrup or something. Not painful, just heavy and slow, like every step takes more effort than it should. So I wouldn’t say I’m moving around more than usual. If anything, I feel physically slowed down, and I think other people can see it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you’re worthless or felt guilty about things more than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. That’s probably one of the things I notice the most, actually.\n\nIt’s not just like, “Oh, I made a mistake, that was embarrassing.” It turns into this whole thing in my head where I keep replaying really small stuff from the lab over and over. Like if I labeled something awkwardly, or missed a step and had to correct it, or asked a question I feel like I should’ve already known the answer to… I’ll think about it later and it feels huge. Way bigger than it probably looked to anyone else.\n\nAnd then I don’t just think, “I’m having a hard time,” or “I need to improve.” I start telling myself I’m bad at the job, and then it kind of keeps going until it’s like… I’m not just bad at the job, I’m bad as a person. Like there’s something fundamentally wrong with me. And if there are consequences, or if people get frustrated with me, part of me feels like I deserve it. Like whatever happens because of it is just what I should expect.\n\nI know that sounds extreme when I say it out loud, especially because some of the mistakes are tiny. But in the moment, it doesn’t feel tiny. It feels like proof."
